pub const CARD: &str = r##"# Actions: declared results and typed server handlers

Actions expose typed operations implemented by the application's TypeScript server handlers.
Use them for work that calls external systems and reads or writes through Quarry operations.

```quarry
use server
action invite(email: text <=200) returns { invitation: id, providerId: text <=100 ? } secrets [MAIL_API_KEY] retry 3 backoff 2s deadline 1h cancellation allow
action refreshModel(periodStart: time, periodEnd: time) returns {} <- viewer = 'service:ranker'
action paused() <- deny
schedule refreshHourly every 1h as service:ranker: refreshModel
```

No Quarry body: the application's TypeScript server handler performs the action.
Arguments are typed like a mutation's. `returns { field: type, ... }` declares
an object of bounded scalar or list fields; table references are IDs, never
rows. Optional result fields become required nullable fields in generated
types; settlement fills omitted optional fields with null. `returns {}`
requires an empty object. Omitting `returns` preserves an `unknown` result.
Query cursors cannot be results. A result failing validation fails terminally,
because external work may already have happened. Return protected content by
managed row ID so current read rules govern it.

`check` and `dev` generate both the client's `Action<A, R>` in `api.ts` and
the handler's `Actions` signatures in `server.ts`, under `snapback/generated/`.
Handlers use flat qualified keys (`"mail.invite"` for an action in `mail.q`);
every declared action needs a handler. If no actions are declared, omit the
`actions` registry; declared HTTP routes use a separate `routes` registry
(guide routes). An optional `<-` rule uses `allow`, `deny` or
comparisons of `viewer` with principal literals (`and`, `or`, `not`);
it cannot read tables or arguments. Actions always run on the server and
never predict. `client.act(api.invite, { email })` returns `Act`:
`done` with `id` and `result`, `failed` with `id` and `why`, or `pending`
with `id`. A refusal before admission is `{ denied, id }` (`E_OFFLINE`
when offline).

```ts
// snapback/server.ts; mail is the application's provider adapter.
import { server } from "./generated/server.ts"; import { startServer } from "snapback4/server";
export default server(startServer, { actions: {
  async invite({ email }, ctx) {
    const sent = await ctx.step("invite", { email }, key =>
      mail.invite(email, { apiKey: ctx.secrets.MAIL_API_KEY, idempotencyKey: key }));
    return { invitation: sent.id, providerId: sent.providerId ?? null };
  },
  async refreshModel({ periodStart, periodEnd }, ctx) { return {}; },
  async paused() { return {}; },
} });
```

`ctx.query` and `ctx.mutate` run under the invocation's authority and return
the ordinary `Read` and `Write` states; no transaction spans two calls.
`ctx.principal`, `ctx.invocation` (id, attempt, deadline and cancellation),
`ctx.secrets`, `ctx.step` and `ctx.writeIdFor(label)` describe that invocation.
`ctx.writeIdFor` derives a stable mutation ID for one label. `ctx.step`
persists an external step's result and reuses it on a rerun; changing the input
for the same name refuses. Send the step's stable key to the provider, and
reconcile an uncertain provider outcome through that key. There is no `ctx.sql`.
The engine keeps one typed transition policy for durable external steps.
Actions use `_action_steps`; effects have their own `_effect_steps` records
and `ctx.step`, without gaining action authority (guide effects).

`secrets [ENV_NAME, ...]` exposes only the declared names on `ctx.secrets`.
Use uppercase environment names; runtime names (`SNAPBACK_*`, `SNAPBACK4_*`,
`NODE_*`, `LD_*`, `DYLD_*`, `LC_*`, `PATH`, `HOME` and environment controls)
are reserved. Duplicates refuse. Each clause appears at most once, before `<-`.
`retry N` counts total attempts including the first (1–100, default 8).
`backoff` starts at 1s–5m (default 2s for actions, 4s for effects), doubles per attempt up to 5m.
`deadline` is 1s–30d from admission (default none); durations are positive
whole numbers with `s`, `m`, `h` or `d`. `cancellation allow|deny` defaults to
allow; cancellation is cooperative for work already in flight. An effect
declaration accepts the same secrets and policy clauses (guide effects).

A scheduled action requires `as service:<seat>` before the schedule's colon.
The only supplied arguments are `periodStart: time` and `periodEnd: time`,
when declared. A job schedule keeps system authority and cannot use schedule-level `as`;
a job body may use a read-only `as principal:` block (guide jobs).
The optional `at HH:MM` comes before `as`; the shortest period remains 1m.

Legacy `runWorker` action handlers receive `(args, meta)` instead of this
generated context. The following session and wire contracts still apply.
`meta.session` is for the REQUEST's principal, so reads and writes obey
the viewer's rules. It cannot refresh or use worker/auth-minting routes.
It expires with the lease (60 seconds by default) and is revoked on
settlement. Set `actionSessions: { invite: 120 }` and `lease: 120` for a
longer handler (maximum 900 seconds; the shorter duration wins, also capped
by the admitting family's live session expiry).
Development clock advances extend held action leases and their viewer
sessions by the advanced duration, capped by the admitting family's expiry;
ordinary sessions keep their expiry.
`meta.id` is the legacy invocation idempotency key. Delivery is at
least once: thrown errors follow the declared retry policy; `GiveUp` fails
immediately. An expired lease follows the same attempt and deadline bounds.
Only the handler names supplied to `runWorker` are claimed.

Admission records the session's family, principal generation and expiry.
Refresh keeps the family; a fresh sign-in starts another. Before a claim,
revoking that session, logout-all, principal revocation or expiry fails the
request terminally with `E_ACTION_AUTHORITY`: "the authority that admitted
this action was revoked or has expired". No delegated session is minted.
Service-session admissions have the same binding. Development personas and
legacy requests without a recorded binding retain their existing behavior.
Inspect recorded steps and provider outcomes before starting new intent under
a new live session; a fresh sign-in does not revive the old admission.

`POST /a/<name> { id, args }` under a live viewer session records the
request durably, once per id, then waits up to 25 seconds. A settled reply
is `{ state: "done", id, result }` or `{ state: "failed", id, why }`.
Otherwise HTTP 202 carries `{ state: "pending", id, why }`, with retryable
`E_ACTION_PENDING`. Repeat with the SAME id to collect the outcome
(`replayed: true`); `client.act(op, args, { id: previous.id })` does this.
An id binds the operation and canonical JSON of raw wire arguments before
validation fills optional nulls (object keys are sorted). Changed arguments or another action refuse `E_ID_REUSED`;
use the original arguments or mint a new id. Another principal gets
`E_ACTION_UNKNOWN`. Mutation ids likewise bind the sent operation name and
canonical raw wire arguments; legacy mutation receipts without a binding replay.
Collecting a result also requires the original family to remain live and
its principal generation unchanged; otherwise `E_ACTION_UNKNOWN` returns
without the result, even after a fresh sign-in by the same principal.
Return protected content by managed row id so current read rules govern it.
`{ waitSeconds: 0 }` (`?wait=0` on the wire) only admits or collects;
the default is 25. A transport loss after submission also returns pending:
the server may already have accepted it. The SDK never queues an action.

Workers send a service bearer token to `POST /actions/claim { action?,
limit?, lease?, wait?, session? }`; durations are seconds. It long-polls
and returns `{ requests: [{ id, action, args, principal, session, attempt,
at }] }`. Settle via `POST /actions/<id>/done { result }` or
`/failed { error, retry }`, with the service bearer and the claim's
`session.token` in `x-snapback-action-lease`. That header fences an old
attempt even when its successor uses the same service token. Stale claims
get `E_ACTION_LEASE`; user tokens get HTTP 401 `E_AUTH`. Results are JSON
bounded by the unchanged 1 MiB output ceiling, and declared result fields are
validated using the same scalar rules as arguments.

## Operations

For additional workers, declare `snapback/workers/<service>.mjs` (also `.mts`
or `.ts`). Both dev and serve run these entries; guide effects shows the
completion ledger and stable provider key. `deploy` packages that source subtree.


`snapback4 dev` starts `snapback/server.ts` (or `.mts` or `.mjs`), falling back
to `snapback/worker.ts` (or `.mts` or `.mjs`), with Node 24, `SNAPBACK_URL`
and `SNAPBACK_SERVICE_TOKEN` for `service:worker`, plus `SNAPBACK_WORKER_LEDGER`
for opt-in low-level effect completions. Tokens are never printed.
`dev --worker` is refused; use one of those conventional paths.
It restarts a crashed worker with backoff and stops it on dev exit.
The child environment is constructed explicitly, with runtime variables and
the declared secrets; a missing secret refuses startup with E_SECRET_MISSING.
Supply the declared environment variable to the host before restarting; keep
its value out of Quarry, generated files and logs.

`snapback4 serve <release-dir> --worker <path>` supervises that Node 24 entry
with the same restart backoff and shutdown. Its environment contains only
`PATH`, `HOME`, `TMPDIR`, `LANG`, `LC_ALL`, `TZ`, the supplied
`SNAPBACK_URL`, `SNAPBACK_SERVICE_TOKEN` and `SNAPBACK_WORKER_LEDGER`, declared
secrets and an internal supervision marker. Parent `NODE_OPTIONS` is not copied. Without --worker, serve starts the captured conventional server/worker entry.
Deployment captures its checked project import graph and generated registry; installed
Node packages remain host dependencies. --worker explicitly overrides that entry. The service:worker token is minted
for each child and never printed by serve. Each child session lasts at most
one day; the supervisor replaces the child within that window. Clean exits
revoke its family. Startup revokes the previous supervised child's family,
even after SIGKILL, without revoking manually issued service seats. The path is resolved from the
command's working directory; the child runs in the active release directory.
`doctor [--release dir]` reports worker starts, restarts and crashes and counts
queued/running/done/failed requests. `why <id> [--release dir]` describes an
action, mutation or effect, including bindings, attempts, settlement and
payload sizes/digests; `--values` also prints stored arguments and results.
Mutation arguments and results are not recorded; their binding keeps only
the operation name, argument digest and admission time beside the receipt.
Install TypeScript 5 or 6 and @types/node in the app (`bun add --dev
typescript@5.9.3 @types/node@24`; TypeScript 7 has no compiler API to load):
`check` and `dev` check the server entry's import graph strictly without
running it. Node 24 requires
extensions on relative TypeScript imports. Work and step records have no
automatic retention expiry; their ids are never silently reusable. Parked
work needs operator reconciliation; there is no resume command in this slice.

const SCHEMA: &str = "use personas alice, bob\nuse identity\ntable items:\n  n: int\n  photo: image <=1024 ?\n  read <- allow\n  insert <- deny\n  sync public\nquery who():\n  return viewer\n";


pub(super) struct Host {
    server: Arc<Server>,
    pub(super) shared: Arc<Shared>,
    admission: Arc<RequestAdmission>,
    worker: Option<JoinHandle<()>>,
    pub(super) address: SocketAddr,
}
impl Host {
    fn new(dev: bool) -> Self {
        Self::with_store(dev, snapback4_core::Store::memory().unwrap(), SCHEMA)
    }

    pub(super) fn with_store(dev: bool, store: snapback4_core::Store, source: &str) -> Self {
        Self::with_store_on(dev, store, source, "127.0.0.1")
    }
    fn with_store_on(dev: bool, store: snapback4_core::Store, source: &str, bind: &str) -> Self {
        Self::with_evidence(dev, store, source, bind, None)
    }
    fn request(&self, method: &str, path: &str, headers: &str, body: &str) -> String {
        let device = if path == "/sync" && !headers.to_ascii_lowercase().contains("x-snapback-device:") {
            format!("x-snapback-device: {}\r\n", serde_json::to_string(&snapback4_core::device_protocol::DeviceEngine::current()).unwrap())
        } else { String::new() };
        let headers = format!("{headers}{device}");
        exchange(
            self.address,
            &format!(
                "{method} {path} HTTP/1.1\r\nHost: localhost\r\nConnection: close\r\nContent-Length: {}\r\n{headers}\r\n{body}",
                body.len()
            ),
        )
    }
}
impl Drop for Host {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        *self.shared.seq.lock().unwrap() = u64::MAX;
        lock(&self.shared.wake).seq=u64::MAX;
        self.shared.committed.notify_all();
        self.shared.polls.notify_all();
        self.server.unblock();
        self.worker.take().unwrap().join().unwrap();
    }
}

pub(super) fn exchange(address: SocketAddr, wire: &str) -> String {
    exchange_bytes(address, wire.as_bytes())
}

pub(crate) fn work_authority_expiry(
    tx: &impl AuthorityTransaction,
    work: &WorkRecord,
    now: i64,
) -> Result<Option<i64>, RuntimeError> {
    if let Some(generation) = work.service_generation {
        return Ok((tx.principal_generation(&work.principal)? == generation).then_some(i64::MAX));
    }
    match (&work.admission.family, work.admission.generation, work.admission.expires_at) {
        (Some(family), Some(generation), Some(_)) => family_authority_expiry(
            tx,
            &work.principal,
            family,
            generation,
            now,
        ),
        _ => Ok(Some(i64::MAX)), // Legacy rows and development personas.
    }
}

fn claim_window_is_live(work: &WorkRecord, now: i64) -> bool {
    work.state == "running"
        && work.lease_until.is_some_and(|lease| lease > now)
        && work.deadline.is_none_or(|deadline| deadline > now)
}

//! Development doors beside the operation routes: `POST /seed`, `POST /dev/reset`, and the
//! `--no-watch` hint that names a schema edited on disk after adoption.
use super::{Shared, lock, refusal_json, respond_timed};
use crate::project::{Project, SourceStamp};
use serde_json::{Value as Json, json};
use snapback4_core::error::Refusal;
use snapback4_core::wire;
use std::collections::BTreeMap;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
use std::sync::Mutex;
use tiny_http::Request;

/// The source stamp each `--no-watch` dev host adopted, by project root.
static PINNED: Mutex<BTreeMap<PathBuf, SourceStamp>> = Mutex::new(BTreeMap::new());

pub(super) fn closed() -> Refusal {
    Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", "development doors are closed; use serve staging with real users (guide staging), or explicitly opt into dev --unsafe-dev-doors for local fixtures").guide("staging")
}

pub(super) fn pin(project: &Path, stamp: SourceStamp) {
    PINNED.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner()).insert(project.to_owned(), stamp);
}

/// A `--no-watch` host serves the schema it started with. When a request
/// names an operation or argument that host does not know and the `.q` files
/// changed on disk since, say so: the edit is real, it was just never adopted.
pub(super) fn stale(shared: &Shared, refusal: Refusal) -> Refusal {
    let Some(adopted) = PINNED.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner()).get(&shared.project).copied() else { return refusal };
    if (Project { root: shared.project.clone() }).sources_stamp() == adopted { return refusal; }
    let message = format!("{}; this dev host runs with --no-watch and snapback/ changed on disk after it adopted its schema, so the edit is not served", refusal.message);
    Refusal { message, rewrite: Some("restart `snapback4 dev` (or drop --no-watch so saves re-adopt), then retry".into()), ..refusal }
}

// @ref LLP 3000.036.002#8 — persona names are not their principals; dev only.
pub(super) fn persona_argument(shared: &Shared, kinds: &BTreeMap<String,snapback4_core::ir::ArgType>, args: &Json) -> Option<Refusal> {
    if !shared.personas { return None; }
    let bound = lock(&shared.bound);
    crate::persona_principals::arguments(&bound.personas,kinds,args)
}
// Rows are written as the system principal; each names its id because seed is an upsert by id.
pub(super) fn seed(request: Request, shared: &Shared, body: &str) {
    if !shared.dev_doors() || !request.remote_addr().is_some_and(|a| a.ip().is_loopback()) {
        respond_timed(request, shared, 403, json!({ "denied": refusal_json(&Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", "seeding is a development door")) }));
        return;
    }
    let refuse = |request, refusal: Refusal| respond_timed(request, shared, 400, json!({ "denied": refusal_json(&refusal.guide("personas")) }));
    let rows: Vec<Json> = match serde_json::from_str(body) {
        Ok(Json::Array(rows)) => rows,
        _ => return refuse(request, Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", "seed takes a JSON array of {table, row}")),
    };
    let mut engine = lock(&shared.engine);
    if let Err(why) = shared.dev_authority(&engine) { drop(engine); return respond_timed(request, shared, 403, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&why)})); }
    let now = shared.now();
    let typed = match typed_rows(engine.schema(), &rows, now) {
        Ok(typed) => typed,
        Err(refusal) => { drop(engine); return refuse(request, refusal); }
    };
    let outcome = engine.seed_at(typed, now);
    drop(engine);
    match outcome {
        Ok(count) => {
            shared.durable_committed(&lock(&shared.engine));
            respond_timed(request, shared, 200, json!({ "seeded": count }));
        }
        Err(error) => respond_timed(request, shared, 200, json!({ "denied": refusal_json(&seed_refusal(error)) })),
    }
}

/// `/seed` rows checked against the served schema and typed, or the first problem.
fn typed_rows(schema: &snapback4_core::Schema, rows: &[Json], now: i64) -> Result<Vec<(String, snapback4_core::Value)>, Refusal> {
    let mut typed = Vec::new();
    for (index, entry) in rows.iter().enumerate() {
        let (Some(table), Some(row)) = (entry.get("table").and_then(Json::as_str), entry.get("row")) else {
            return Err(Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", "each seed entry is {table, row}"));
        };
        let Some(definition) = schema.table(table) else {
            return Err(Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("unknown table {table}")));
        };
        if row.is_object() && row.get("id").is_none_or(|id| !id.is_string()) {
            return Err(missing_id(index, table));
        }
        let mut row = row.clone();
        crate::verbs::test_fixture::relative_times(definition, &mut row, now)
            .map_err(|why| Refusal::new("E_FIXTURE_TIME", "input", format!("seed row {index} ({table}): {why}")))?;
        if let Some(why) = wire::bare_principal(definition, &row) {
            return Err(Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("seed row {index} ({table}): {why}")));
        }
        match wire::row_from_json(definition, &row) {
            Ok(value) => typed.push((table.to_owned(), value)),
            Err(why) => return Err(Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("{table}: {why}"))),
        }
    }
    Ok(typed)
}

fn seed_refusal(error: snapback4_core::RuntimeError) -> Refusal {
    let mut refusal = error.refusal();
    // Reseeding with changed immutable values reads like a rule problem; say what happened.
    if refusal.code == "E_CONSTRAINT" && refusal.message.ends_with(" is immutable") {
        refusal.message.push_str("; /seed is an upsert by id, and a row with that id already exists with a different value in that column. Reseed it with its original value, give the row a new id, POST /dev/reset first, or restart dev with --memory for an empty store");
    }
    refusal
}

/// What `POST /dev/reset` was asked to do: the body is empty, a `/seed`
/// array, or `{"seed": [..], "clock": "reset"}` with either key optional.
#[derive(Debug, Default, PartialEq)]
struct ResetRequest { seed: Vec<Json>, clock: bool }

fn reset_request(body: &str) -> Result<ResetRequest, Refusal> {
    const SHAPE: &str = "reset takes no body, a /seed array [{table, row}, ..], or {\"seed\": [..], \"clock\": \"reset\"} with either key optional";
    if body.trim().is_empty() { return Ok(ResetRequest::default()); }
    match serde_json::from_str(body) {
        Ok(Json::Array(seed)) => Ok(ResetRequest { seed, clock: false }),
        Ok(Json::Object(fields)) => {
            let mut request = ResetRequest::default();
            for (key, value) in fields {
                match (key.as_str(), value) {
                    ("seed", Json::Array(seed)) => request.seed = seed,
                    ("clock", Json::String(clock)) if clock == "reset" => request.clock = true,
                    ("clock", Json::String(clock)) if clock == "keep" => request.clock = false,
                    ("clock", other) => return Err(crate::dev_clock::input(format!("reset's clock is \"reset\" (back to wall time) or \"keep\" (the default), not {other}"))),
                    (key, _) => return Err(Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("{SHAPE}; got key {key:?}"))),
                }
            }
            Ok(request)
        }
        _ => Err(Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", SHAPE)),
    }
}

   104	}

   106	pub(super) fn claim(request: Request, shared: &Shared, body: &str) {
   107	    claim_selected(request, shared, body, false)
   108	}
   109	
   110	pub(super) fn claim_set(request: Request, shared: &Shared, body: &str) {
   111	    claim_selected(request, shared, body, true)
   112	}
   113	
   114	fn claim_selected(request: Request, shared: &Shared, body: &str, registry: bool) {
   115	    let payload: Json = match serde_json::from_str(body) { Ok(Json::Object(p)) => Json::Object(p), _ => return respond(request, 400, json!({ "denied": Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", "claim takes an object") })) };
   116	    let names: Option<Vec<String>> = if registry {
   117	        // The existing whole-request byte bound applies. Do not shorten the
   118	        // accepted operation-name surface: private route names have a prefix.
   119	        let names = payload.get("actions").and_then(Json::as_array).and_then(|values| values.iter().map(|value| value.as_str().filter(|name| !name.is_empty()).map(str::to_owned)).collect::<Option<Vec<_>>>());
   120	        match names {
   121	            Some(names) if !names.is_empty() && names.len() <= 128 && payload.get("action").is_none() => Some(names),
   122	            _ => return respond(request, 400, json!({ "denied": Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", "claim-set takes actions: 1–128 nonempty names, without action") })),
   123	        }
   124	    } else { None };
   125	    let action = payload.get("action").and_then(Json::as_str);
   126	    let limit = payload.get("limit").and_then(Json::as_u64).unwrap_or(1).clamp(1, 100) as usize;
   127	    let lease = payload.get("lease").and_then(Json::as_i64).unwrap_or(60).clamp(1, 900) * 1_000;
   128	    let session = payload.get("session").and_then(Json::as_i64).unwrap_or(SESSION_SECONDS);
   129	    let deadline = Instant::now() + Duration::from_secs(payload.get("wait").and_then(Json::as_u64).unwrap_or(0).min(25));
   130	    loop {
   131	        let (worker, claimed) = {
   132	            let mut engine = producing_engine(shared);
   133	            let worker = match service_of_locked(&request, shared, &engine) { Ok(worker) => worker, Err(why) => return respond(request, 401, json!({ "denied": why })) };
   134	            let claimed = match &names {
   135	                Some(names) => engine.claim_action_set(&worker, names, limit, lease, session, shared.now()),
   136	                None => engine.claim_actions(&worker, action, limit, lease, session, shared.now()),
   137	            };
   138	            (worker, claimed)
   139	        };
   140	        if claimed.as_ref().is_ok_and(|runs| !runs.is_empty()) { shared.durable_committed(&lock(&shared.engine)); }
   141	        match claimed {
   142	            Ok(claims) if claims.is_empty() && Instant::now() < deadline => {
   143	                let seq = lock(&shared.seq);
   144	                let _ = shared.committed.wait_timeout(seq, deadline.saturating_duration_since(Instant::now()).min(Duration::from_secs(1))).unwrap_or_else(|p| p.into_inner());
   145	            }
   146	            Ok(claims) => return respond(request, 200, json!({ "requests": claims.iter().map(claim_json).collect::<Vec<_>>(), "worker": worker })),
   147	            Err(error) => return respond(request, if error.refusal().retryable { 503 } else { 200 }, json!({ "denied": failure(&error) })),
   148	        }
   149	    }
   150	}
   151	
   152	pub(super) fn settle(request: Request, shared: &Shared, path: &str, body: &str) {
   153	    if let Some(id) = path.strip_prefix("/actions/").and_then(|path| path.strip_suffix("/cancel")).and_then(decode_id) {
   154	        let principal = match principal_of(&request, shared) { Ok(p) => p.0, Err(why) => return respond(request, 401, json!({ "denied": why })) };
   155	        let answer = producing_engine(shared).cancel_action(&id, &principal, shared.now());
   156	        return match answer {
   157	            Ok(run) => { shared.durable_committed(&lock(&shared.engine)); respond(request, 200, json!({ "ok": true, "request": outcome(&run, false) })); }
   158	            Err(error) => respond(request, if error.refusal().retryable { 503 } else { 200 }, json!({ "denied": failure(&error) })),
   159	        };
   160	    }
   161	    let worker = match service_of(&request, shared) { Ok(worker) => worker, Err(why) => return respond(request, 401, json!({ "denied": why })) };
   162	    let token = match single_header(&request, "x-snapback-action-lease") { Ok(Some(token)) => token.to_owned(), _ => return respond(request, 200, json!({ "denied": Refusal::new("E_ACTION_LEASE", "action", "settle with the claim's session.token in x-snapback-action-lease").guide("actions") })) };
   163	    let payload: Json = match serde_json::from_str(body) { Ok(Json::Object(p)) => Json::Object(p), _ => return respond(request, 400, json!({ "denied": Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", "settlement takes { result } or { error, retry }") })) };
   164	    if let Some(tail) = path.strip_prefix("/actions/") {
   165	        if let Some(id) = tail.strip_suffix("/status").and_then(decode_id) {
   166	            let answer = producing_engine(shared).action_status(&id, &worker, &token, shared.now());
   167	            return match answer {
   168	                Ok(run) => respond(request, 200, json!({ "invocation": { "id": run.id, "attempt": run.attempt, "deadline": run.deadline, "cancelled": run.cancelled, "state": run.state, "errorCode": run.error_code } })),
   169	                Err(error) => respond(request, if error.refusal().retryable { 503 } else { 200 }, json!({ "denied": failure(&error) })),
   170	            };
   171	        }
   172	        if let Some((id, tail)) = tail.split_once("/steps/") {
   173	            if let Some((name, verb)) = tail.rsplit_once('/') {
   174	                if let (Some(id), Some(name)) = (decode_id(id), decode_id(name)) {
   175	                    let field = match verb { "begin" => Some("input"), "done" => Some("result"), _ => None };
   176	                    if field.is_some_and(|field| payload.get(field).is_none()) {
   177	                        return respond(request, 400, json!({ "denied": Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", "a step begin requires input and a step done requires result (null is explicit)") }));
   178	                    }
   179	                    let answer = {
   180	                        let mut engine = producing_engine(shared);
   181	                        match verb {
   182	                            "begin" => engine.begin_action_step(&id, &worker, &token, &name, payload.get("input").unwrap_or(&Json::Null), payload.get("reconcile").and_then(Json::as_bool).unwrap_or(false), shared.now()).map(|step| json!({ "step": step })),
   183	                            "done" => engine.complete_action_step(&id, &worker, &token, &name, payload.get("result").unwrap_or(&Json::Null), shared.now()).map(|step| json!({ "ok": true, "step": step })),
   184	                            "park" => engine.park_action_step(&id, &worker, &token, &name, payload.get("error").and_then(Json::as_str).unwrap_or("the provider outcome is unknown"), shared.now()).map(|()| json!({ "ok": true })),
   185	                            _ => Err(snapback4_core::RuntimeError::Input("step routes are begin, done or park".into())),
   186	                        }
   187	                    };
   188	                    shared.durable_committed(&lock(&shared.engine));
   189	                    return match answer {
   190	                        Ok(answer) => respond(request, 200, answer),
   191	                        Err(error) => respond(request, if error.refusal().retryable { 503 } else { 200 }, json!({ "denied": failure(&error) })),
   192	                    };
   193	                }
   194	            }
   195	        }
   196	    }
   197	    let (id, done) = if let Some(id) = path.strip_prefix("/actions/").and_then(|p| p.strip_suffix("/done")) { (id, true) }
   198	        else if let Some(id) = path.strip_prefix("/actions/").and_then(|p| p.strip_suffix("/failed")) { (id, false) }
   199	        else { return respond(request, 404, json!({ "denied": Refusal::new("E_ACTION_UNKNOWN", "action", "no action settlement at this path") })); };
   200	    let settled = if done { ActionSettled::Done(payload.get("result").cloned().unwrap_or(Json::Null)) }
   201	        else {
   202	            let error = payload.get("error").and_then(Json::as_str).unwrap_or("the handler failed").to_owned();
   203	            let retry = payload.get("retry").and_then(Json::as_bool).unwrap_or(true);
   204	            let code = match payload.get("code").and_then(Json::as_str) {
   205	                Some("E_SECRET_MISSING") => Some("E_SECRET_MISSING"), Some("E_ACTION_GENERATION") => Some("E_ACTION_GENERATION"),
   206	                Some("E_ACTION_RESULT") => Some("E_ACTION_RESULT"), Some("E_STEP_INPUT") => Some("E_STEP_INPUT"),
   207	                Some("E_ROUTE_RESULT") => Some("E_ROUTE_RESULT"),
   208	                Some("E_STEP_RESULT") => Some("E_STEP_RESULT"), Some("E_STEP_PARKED") => Some("E_STEP_PARKED"),
   209	                Some("E_ACTION_CANCELLED") => Some("E_ACTION_CANCELLED"), Some("E_ACTION_DEADLINE") => Some("E_ACTION_DEADLINE"),
   210	                Some("E_ACTION_CLOSED") => Some("E_ACTION_CLOSED"), Some("E_WRITE_LABEL") => Some("E_WRITE_LABEL"),
   211	                Some("E_OIDC_BINDING") => Some("E_OIDC_BINDING"), Some("E_OIDC_CALLBACK") => Some("E_OIDC_CALLBACK"),
   212	                Some("E_OIDC_CONFIG") => Some("E_OIDC_CONFIG"), Some("E_OIDC_EXCHANGE") => Some("E_OIDC_EXCHANGE"),
   213	                Some("E_OIDC_HANDOFF") => Some("E_OIDC_HANDOFF"), Some("E_OIDC_MINT") => Some("E_OIDC_MINT"),
   214	                Some("E_OIDC_PROVIDER") => Some("E_OIDC_PROVIDER"), Some("E_OIDC_RECIPE") => Some("E_OIDC_RECIPE"),
   215	                Some("E_OIDC_REPLAY") => Some("E_OIDC_REPLAY"), Some("E_OIDC_TRANSACTION") => Some("E_OIDC_TRANSACTION"),
   216	                _ => None,
   217	            };
   218	            if !retry && code.is_some() { ActionSettled::Refused { code: code.unwrap(), error } } else { ActionSettled::Failed { error, retry } }
   219	        };
   220	    let Some(id) = decode_id(id) else { return respond(request, 400, json!({ "denied": Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", "the action id must be percent-encoded UTF-8") })); };
   221	    let answer = producing_engine(shared).settle_action(&id, &worker, &token, settled, shared.now());
   222	    match answer {
   223	        Ok(run) => {
   224	            if !done && shared.personas { snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4: action {} {} attempt {} settled {}: {}", run.action, run.id, run.attempt, run.state, serde_json::to_string(&run.error).unwrap_or_default()); }
   225	            shared.durable_committed(&lock(&shared.engine)); respond(request, 200, json!({ "ok": true, "request": outcome(&run, false) }));
   226	        }
   227	        Err(error) => { let why = failure(&error); respond(request, if why.retryable { 503 } else { 200 }, json!({ "denied": why })); }
   228	    }
   229	}

 succeeded in 0ms:
//! The CLI's local service-seat door. This capability never comes from a bearer session.
use super::{Shared, single_header, authority_host, producing_engine, respond_with_cors};
use snapback4_core::{error::Refusal, identity::{self, Session}};
use std::{io::{self, Read, Seek, SeekFrom, Write}, net::TcpStream, path::{Path, PathBuf}, time::Duration};
use serde_json::{Value as Json, json};
use tiny_http::{Method, Request};

pub(crate) const ENDPOINT: &str = "/dev/token";
#[path = "serve_token_protocol.rs"]
mod protocol;
use protocol::{Mint, CLIENT, SERVER, PROOF};
pub(crate) use protocol::Authority;
const BODY_LIMIT: usize = 4096;
const SECRET_LEN: usize = 64;

fn secret_path(store: &Path) -> PathBuf {
    let mut path = store.as_os_str().to_owned();
    path.push("-token");
    path.into()
}

// @ref LLP 3000.019#3.4 — preserve the hardened auxiliary-file checks and the single owner's lifetime.
pub(crate) fn publish(store: &Path) -> io::Result<Authority> {
    #[cfg(unix)] {
        let secret = protocol::hex(&protocol::random()?);
        let mut file = snapback4_core::private_fs::auxiliary_file(store, &secret_path(store), true, SECRET_LEN as u64)?;
        file.set_len(0)?;
        file.seek(SeekFrom::Start(0))?;
        file.write_all(secret.as_bytes())?;
        Ok(Authority::new(secret, protocol::store_identity(store)?))
    }
    #[cfg(not(unix))] {
        let _ = store;
        Err(io::Error::other("local service minting requires Unix private files"))
    }
}

fn read_secret(store: &Path) -> io::Result<String> {
    #[cfg(unix)] {
        use std::os::unix::fs::PermissionsExt;
        let file = snapback4_core::private_fs::auxiliary_file(store, &secret_path(store), false, SECRET_LEN as u64)?;
        if file.metadata()?.permissions().mode() & 0o777 != 0o600 {
            return Err(io::Error::other("the store token proof must have mode 0600"));
        }
        let mut secret = String::new();
        file.take((SECRET_LEN + 1) as u64).read_to_string(&mut secret)?;
        if secret.len() != SECRET_LEN || !secret.bytes().all(|c| c.is_ascii_hexdigit()) {
            return Err(io::Error::other("the store token proof is incomplete"));
        }
        Ok(secret)
    }
    #[cfg(not(unix))] {
        let _ = store;
        Err(io::Error::other("local service minting requires Unix private files"))
    }
}

fn refusal(code: &str, family: &str, message: &str, rewrite: &str) -> Refusal {
    Refusal::new(code, family, message).site(ENDPOINT).rewrite(rewrite).guide("services")
}

pub(super) fn denied(request: Request) {
    respond_with_cors(request, 403, json!({"denied": refusal("E_AUTH", "auth",
        "service token minting requires a loopback peer and this server's private store proof",
        "run snapback4 token --service <name> on the server host as the store owner (add --release <dir> for serve)")}), false);
}

// @ref LLP 3000.019#3.4 — authenticate both ends before minting; the store key never leaves its host file/memory.
pub(super) fn handle(mut request: Request, shared: &Shared) {
    let local = request.remote_addr().is_some_and(|peer| peer.ip().is_loopback())
        && matches!(single_header(&request, "host"), Ok(Some(host)) if authority_host(host)
            .is_some_and(|host| host.parse::<std::net::IpAddr>().is_ok_and(|ip| ip.is_loopback())))
        && matches!(single_header(&request, "origin"), Ok(None))
        && matches!(single_header(&request, "x-snapback-store-secret"), Ok(None));
    let Some(authority) = shared.owner.as_ref().and_then(|o| o.token_authority.as_ref()).filter(|_| local) else {
        return denied(request);
    };
    if request.method() != &Method::Post || request.url() != ENDPOINT
        || request.body_length().is_some_and(|n| n > BODY_LIMIT) { return denied(request); }
    let client = match single_header(&request, CLIENT) {
        Ok(Some(value)) => match protocol::nonce(value) { Some(nonce) => nonce, None => return denied(request) },
        _ => return denied(request),
    };
    let (server, proof) = match (single_header(&request, SERVER), single_header(&request, PROOF)) {
        (Ok(None), Ok(None)) => (None, None),
        (Ok(Some(server)), Ok(Some(proof))) => match protocol::nonce(server) {
            Some(server) => (Some(server), Some(proof.to_owned())), None => return denied(request),
        },
        _ => return denied(request),
    };
    let mut bytes = Vec::new();
    if request.as_reader().take((BODY_LIMIT + 1) as u64).read_to_end(&mut bytes).is_err() || bytes.len() > BODY_LIMIT {
        return denied(request);
    }
    let mint: Mint = match serde_json::from_slice::<Mint>(&bytes) {
        Ok(mint) if mint.store == authority.store => mint,
        _ => return denied(request),
    };
    let binding = mint.binding();
    let Some(server) = server else {
        let Some(server) = authority.challenge(client, binding.clone(), std::time::Instant::now()) else { return denied(request); };
        return respond_with_cors(request, 200, json!({"server_nonce": protocol::hex(&server),
            "server_proof": protocol::server_proof(&authority.secret, &client, &server, &binding)}), false);
    };
    if !authority.consume(&client, &server, &binding, proof.as_deref().unwrap(), std::time::Instant::now()) {
        return denied(request);
    }
    let result = {
        // Use the same writer and identity routine as offline minting. Sample
        // wall time as the offline CLI does, independent of dev's test clock.
        let engine = producing_engine(shared);
        shared.owner.as_ref().expect("authorized owner").path().and_then(|_| {
            identity::service_session(engine.store(), &mint.service, snapback4_core::now_ms(), mint.days)
        })
    };
    let reply = match result {
        Ok(session) => json!({"session": session}),
        Err(error) => {
            let why = error.refusal().site(ENDPOINT).rewrite(if matches!(error, snapback4_core::RuntimeError::Auth(_)) {
                "use --service with 1–64 letters, digits, _ or -"
            } else { "inspect the store error, repair the store access, then rerun snapback4 token" }).guide("services");
            json!({"denied": why})
        }
    };
    let Some(sealed) = protocol::seal(&authority.secret, &client, &server, &binding, &serde_json::to_vec(&reply).unwrap()) else {
        return denied(request);
    };
    respond_with_cors(request, 200, json!({"sealed": sealed}), false);
}

fn exchange(address: std::net::SocketAddr, headers: &str, body: &str) -> io::Result<Json> {
    let mut stream = TcpStream::connect_timeout(&address, Duration::from_secs(1))?;
    stream.set_read_timeout(Some(Duration::from_secs(10)))?;
    stream.set_write_timeout(Some(Duration::from_secs(2)))?;
    // HTTP/1.0 keeps the bounded reply length-delimited. No proxy, DNS or redirects.
    write!(stream, "POST {ENDPOINT} HTTP/1.0\r\nHost: {address}\r\n{headers}Content-Type: application/json\r\nConnection: close\r\nContent-Length: {}\r\n\r\n{body}", body.len())?;
    let mut response = String::new();
    stream.take(8193).read_to_string(&mut response)?;
    response.split_once("\r\n\r\n").filter(|_| response.len() <= 8192
        && (response.starts_with("HTTP/1.0 200 ") || response.starts_with("HTTP/1.1 200 ")))
        .and_then(|(_, body)| serde_json::from_str(body).ok())
        .ok_or_else(|| io::Error::other("invalid local mint reply"))
}

// @ref LLP 3000.019#3.4 — busy token commands ask the announced loopback owner, never open a second writer.
pub(crate) fn mint_live(path: &Path, name: &str, days: i64) -> Result<Session, (i32, String)> {
    let store = path.canonicalize().unwrap_or_else(|_| path.to_owned());
    let owner = crate::store_owner::address(&store).unwrap_or_else(|| "another CLI command or a server starting up (address pending)".into());
    let unavailable = |reason: &str| (2, format!("E_STORE_BUSY at {}: owned by {owner}; {reason}. Restore access to that server's loopback listener and private store proof, then rerun `snapback4 token --service <name>` (with the same --release <dir> for serve). For an older or unreachable server, stop it, rerun token offline, then restart it; for another CLI, wait for it to finish. Leave <database>-owner in place", path.display()));
    let address = crate::store_owner::inspector_address(&store).ok_or_else(|| unavailable("no published loopback listener; no mint request sent"))?;
    let secret = read_secret(&store).map_err(|_| unavailable("cannot read the 0600 store token proof; no mint request sent"))?;
    let mint = Mint { service: name.into(), days, store: protocol::store_identity(&store)
        .map_err(|_| unavailable("cannot identify the owned store; no mint request sent"))? };
    let body = serde_json::to_string(&mint).unwrap();
    if body.len() > BODY_LIMIT { return Err((1, "token: E_AUTH at --service: a service name is 1–64 letters, digits, _ or -; use --service worker".into())); }
    let binding = mint.binding();
    let client = protocol::random().map_err(|_| unavailable("cannot create a fresh challenge; no mint request sent"))?;
    let headers = format!("{CLIENT}: {}\r\n", protocol::hex(&client));
    let challenge = exchange(address, &headers, &body).map_err(|_| unavailable("server challenge unavailable; no mint proof sent"))?;
    let server = challenge["server_nonce"].as_str().and_then(protocol::nonce)
        .ok_or_else(|| unavailable("invalid server challenge; no mint proof sent"))?;
    if !challenge["server_proof"].as_str().is_some_and(|proof| protocol::verify_server(&secret, &client, &server, &binding, proof)) {
        return Err(unavailable("server proof did not verify; no mint proof sent"));
    }
    // Only a verified server receives a client proof. Neither message contains the key.
    let headers = format!("{headers}{SERVER}: {}\r\n{PROOF}: {}\r\n", protocol::hex(&server),
        protocol::client_proof(&secret, &client, &server, &binding));
    let reply = exchange(address, &headers, &body)
        .map_err(|_| unavailable("mint reply unavailable; a session may have been minted, but no token was received"))?;
    let reply: Json = reply["sealed"].as_str().and_then(|sealed| protocol::open(&secret, &client, &server, &binding, sealed))
        .and_then(|bytes| serde_json::from_slice(&bytes).ok())
        .ok_or_else(|| unavailable("server returned an invalid mint reply; a session may have been minted"))?;
    if let Some(denied) = reply.get("denied") {
        if let Ok(why) = serde_json::from_value::<Refusal>(denied.clone()) {
            return Err((1, format!("token: {} at {ENDPOINT}: {}; {}", why.code, why.message, why.rewrite.unwrap_or_else(|| "rerun snapback4 token with valid service arguments".into()))));
        }
        return Err(unavailable("server refused the local mint request; restart it with this CLI and retry"));
    }
    let session = &reply["session"];
    let principal = format!("service:{name}");
    let token = session["token"].as_str().filter(|t| t.len() == 43 && t.bytes().all(|c| c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || b"_-".contains(&c)));
    if session["principal"] != principal || session["kind"] != "service" || token.is_none() || session["expires_at"].as_i64().is_none() {
        return Err(unavailable("server returned an invalid mint reply; a session may have been minted"));
    }
    Ok(Session { scope: snapback4_core::identity::SessionScope::Full, cap_expires_at: None, principal, kind: "service".into(), token: token.unwrap().into(), expires_at: session["expires_at"].as_i64().unwrap() })
}

//! HTTP identity admission. Password work owns a bounded KDF slot, never
//! the engine guard. Rate keys use the socket peer, never forwarded headers.
use crate::serve::{Shared, principal_of, refusal_json, respond_timed};
use serde_json::{Value as Json, json};
use sha2::{Digest, Sha256};
use snapback4_core::error::Refusal;
use snapback4_core::{RuntimeError, identity};
use std::collections::HashMap;
use std::sync::{
    Mutex,
    atomic::{AtomicUsize, Ordering},
};
use std::time::{Duration, Instant};
use tiny_http::Request;

pub const BODY_CEILING: usize = 4096;
const WINDOW: Duration = Duration::from_secs(60);
const KEYS: usize = 4096;

pub struct Admission {
    attempts: Mutex<HashMap<String, (Instant, u32)>>,
    kdfs: AtomicUsize,
    dev_kdf_slots: usize,
}
impl Default for Admission {
    fn default() -> Self {
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#14 — ruling 9: dev loopback KDF work fits the host, bounded at 2–16 slots
        let dev_kdf_slots = std::thread::available_parallelism().map(|n| n.get().clamp(2, 16)).unwrap_or(2);
        Self { attempts: Mutex::new(HashMap::new()), kdfs: AtomicUsize::new(0), dev_kdf_slots }
    }
}
impl Admission {
    pub fn kdf_slots(&self, dev_loopback: bool) -> usize {
        if dev_loopback { self.dev_kdf_slots } else { 2 }
    }

    fn attempt(&self, peer: &str, email: Option<&str>, now: Instant) -> bool {
        let mut attempts = self.attempts.lock().unwrap();
        attempts.retain(|_, (start, _)| now.duration_since(*start) < WINDOW);
        let mut keys = vec![(format!("ip:{peer}"), 30)];
        if let Some(email) = email {
            keys.push((format!("email:{}", identity::normalized_email(email)), 10));
        }
        // Charge every attempt, including denials. A full table refuses new
        // keys until expiry rather than evicting a key under attack.
        let mut admitted = true;
        for (key, limit) in keys {
            // A peer that is already over budget may charge existing email
            // keys, but cannot fill the shared map by rotating fresh emails.
            if !admitted && !attempts.contains_key(&key) {
                continue;
            }
            if !attempts.contains_key(&key) && attempts.len() >= KEYS {
                admitted = false;
                continue;
            }
            let (_, count) = attempts.entry(key).or_insert((now, 0));
            *count = count.saturating_add(1);
            admitted &= *count <= limit;
        }
        admitted
    }
    fn kdf(&self, dev_loopback: bool) -> Option<Kdf<'_>> {
        let slots = self.kdf_slots(dev_loopback);
        self.kdfs
            .fetch_update(Ordering::AcqRel, Ordering::Acquire, |n| {
                (n < slots).then_some(n + 1)
            })
            .ok()?;
        Some(Kdf(&self.kdfs))
    }
}
struct Kdf<'a>(&'a AtomicUsize);
impl Drop for Kdf<'_> {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        self.0.fetch_sub(1, Ordering::Release);
    }
}
fn limited(request: Request, shared: &Shared) {
    let mut refusal = Refusal::new(
        "E_RATE_LIMIT",
        "auth",
        "too many identity requests; retry later",
    )
    .guide("personas");
    refusal.retryable = true;
    respond_timed(request, shared, 429, json!({ "denied": refusal_json(&refusal) }));
}

pub fn handle(request: Request, shared: &Shared, path: &str, body: &str) {
    let payload: Json = match serde_json::from_str(if body.is_empty() { "{}" } else { body }) {
        Ok(value) => value,
        Err(_) => {
            return respond_timed(request, shared,
                400,
                json!({ "denied": Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", "identity takes a JSON object") }),
            );
        }
    };
    let email = payload.get("email").and_then(Json::as_str).unwrap_or("");
    let password = payload.get("password").and_then(Json::as_str).unwrap_or("");
    let password_route = matches!(path, "/auth/login" | "/auth/signup");
    let peer = request
        .remote_addr()
        .map(|a| a.ip().to_string())
        .unwrap_or_else(|| "unknown".into());
    let rate_route = matches!(path, "/auth/login" | "/auth/signup" | "/auth/guest");
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#8 — dev loopback has no minute counters; KDF slots remain
    let dev_loopback = shared.personas && request.remote_addr().is_some_and(|peer| peer.ip().is_loopback());
    if rate_route && !dev_loopback
        && !shared
            .auth
            .attempt(&peer, password_route.then_some(email), Instant::now())
    {
        return limited(request, shared);
    }
    let kdf = if password_route {
        match shared.auth.kdf(dev_loopback) {
            Some(permit) => Some(permit),
            None => return limited(request, shared),
        }
    } else {
        None
    };
    let scope = match payload.get("scope") {
        None => Ok(identity::SessionScope::Full),
        Some(Json::String(value)) if value == "full" => Ok(identity::SessionScope::Full),
        Some(Json::String(value)) if value == "read" => Ok(identity::SessionScope::Read),
        _ => Err(RuntimeError::Input("scope must be full or read".into())),
    };
    let outcome = scope.and_then(|scope| perform(&request, shared, path, email, password, scope));
    drop(kdf);
    match outcome {
        Ok(Outcome::Refused(why)) => respond_refused(request, shared, why),
        Ok(Outcome::SupervisedRefresh) => respond_timed(request, shared, 403, json!({ "denied": refusal_json(&Refusal::new(
            "E_WORKER_REFRESH", "auth", "supervised worker tokens are rotated by the supervisor; keep using the injected token",
        ).guide("effects")) })),
        Ok(Outcome::Logout) => respond_timed(request, shared, 200, json!({ "ok": true })),
        Ok(Outcome::LogoutAll(revoked)) => {
            respond_timed(request, shared, 200, json!({ "ok": true, "revoked": revoked }))
        }
        Ok(Outcome::Session(session)) => {
            let mut wire = json!({ "principal": session.principal, "kind": session.kind, "token": session.token, "expiresAt": session.expires_at, "scope": session.scope });
            if let Some(cap) = session.cap_expires_at { wire["capExpiresAt"] = json!(cap); }
            respond_timed(request, shared, 200, json!({ "session": wire }));
        },
        Err(error) => {
            if matches!(path, "/auth/logout" | "/auth/narrow" | "/auth/refresh" | "/auth/logout-all")
                && let RuntimeError::Auth(message) = &error {
                return respond_refused(request, shared, Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", message).guide("personas"));
            }
            let status = error_status(&error);
            respond_timed(request, shared, status, json!({ "denied": refusal_json(&error.refusal()) }));
        }
    }
}

enum Outcome {
    Refused(Refusal),
    Session(identity::Session),
    SupervisedRefresh,
    Logout,
    LogoutAll(u64),
}

pub(crate) fn error_status(error: &RuntimeError) -> u16 {
    if matches!(error, RuntimeError::Auth(_)) { 401 }
    else if error.is_refusal() { 200 } else { 503 }
}

//! Identity and developer inspection.
pub(super) const CARD: &str = r##"# Personas

Every call carries a principal; public visitors use guest sessions; `serve` accepts bearers only. Personas are named development users; password accounts work headlessly in Node and browsers.

```quarry
use personas alice, bob
use identity password

query whoAmI():
  return viewer
```

/auth/config includes development true on dev, false on serve; hide persona controls unless devDoors is true. An email-and-password product declares `use identity password`; plain `use identity` also mints guest sessions.
`use identity guests` admits only guests; without `use identity`, both methods are disabled.

## Seed accounts headlessly

Run `snapback4 check` then `snapback4 dev` with the schema above. Save as `seed.mjs` beside `snapback/`.
Set `SEED_PASSWORD` privately and run `node seed.mjs` (Node 24). Signup returns a session; use `SEED_AUTH=login` for existing accounts (duplicate signup refuses).

```js
import { signup, login } from "snapback4/auth";
import { createClient } from "snapback4/client";
import { api } from "./snapback/generated/api.ts";
const url = process.env.SNAPBACK_URL ?? "http://127.0.0.1:4400";
const password = process.env.SEED_PASSWORD;
if (!password) throw Error("Set SEED_PASSWORD");
const mode = process.env.SEED_AUTH ?? "signup";
if (mode !== "signup" && mode !== "login") throw Error("SEED_AUTH must be signup or login");
const authenticate = mode === "signup" ? signup : login;
const result = await authenticate(url, "seed1@example.test", password);
if (!result.ok) throw Error(result.why.message);
const client = createClient({ url, session: result.session });
try {
  const me = await client.query(api.whoAmI, {});
  if ("denied" in me) throw Error(me.denied.message);
  console.log(client.viewer()); // Only the principal, never the bearer.
} finally { await client.signOut(); }
```

Use `client.mutate(api.operation, args)` for writes; require `state: "sent"` or handle `"failed"` with `why`;
`"pending"` is not success. Use one client per account. The social starter's `scripts/seed.mjs` creates N users
and profiles; replay adapters use the same path. No browser, borrowed token, persona or `/seed` is needed.
SDK calls POST `{email, password}` to `/auth/signup` or `/auth/login`: `{ok: true, session}` or `{ok: false, why}`
(wire: `{session}` or `{denied}`). Use HTTPS off loopback. There is no `snapback4 auth create`; `run --as` and `/seed` do not create password credentials.

Ordinary guest and password sessions last seven days. `refreshSession` atomically exchanges a live token;
`logoutAll` revokes every session for the principal. The client refreshes near expiry and uses an in-memory
session store by default. Both online and local clients expose `session()`, `onSession`, `refreshSession()` and
`logoutAll()`: rotation adopts the bearer before notification, and sign-out or a current-session refusal clears
it. Local `signOut()` fences late refresh responses and completes only after persisted credentials are removed.
Remote logout is best effort and does not gate that local boundary. An in-flight rotation may return its
successor for logout, but cannot adopt or persist it after sign-out. Storage failure rejects sign-out for retry;
`close()` alone preserves the session. Neither operation erases the outbox. Personas have no session; a
bare-token online client has none until refresh returns one. Only the local client has `sync()`.
`localStorageSessionStore()` is an explicit compatibility choice whose token can be read by same-origin script;
native apps should pass their secure store.

Trusted dev E_AUTH explains expiry or possible causes for unknown tokens; the SDK keeps the message.
Sign in again and discard earlier-store tokens. Serve and untrusted dev requests keep causes generic.
See `guide services` for diagnostic limits and `guide jobs` for isolated time-travel tests.

## Operations
`use personas alice, bob` declares a development cast. `snapback4 run send '{..}' --as alice` (the default
persona is alice) and the `x-snapback-persona: alice` header in development act as `dev:alice`; `persona: "alice"` on the client sends the header. A React/Expo dev UI keeps `AppProvider` and `useAuth` with
`createApp({ url, persona: "alice", device })`, one app per persona (`usePersonaApp(persona, openApp)` opens it and closes it on switch; web `?as=` and React Native pickers: "Personas in a React UI" in CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md;
`init --web`/`--expo` writes one beside a schema without `use identity`, and `init --adapt` rewrites an unmodified guest starter screen into one). No session: `app.auth` sign-in, `auth.guest()` too, refuses `E_AUTH`.
N_PERSONA_PRINCIPAL: declared personas use dev:NAME. The declaration is the cast: dev refuses a persona it does not name with 401 E_AUTH, naming the line to extend, so
an acceptance test can expect an outsider to be refused (a schema without `use personas` admits no persona). Personas are a development door: the socket peer, `Host`, and optional
browser `Origin` must all be literal loopback/local http(s) values. Foreign, opaque, malformed and duplicate
Origins are refused before routing, and CORS reflects only an admitted local Origin. `snapback4 dev` is
development only: password-only apps close fixture, clock and persona doors by default;
`--unsafe-dev-doors` explicitly enables the unauthenticated local tools. Other development schemas keep
the declared cast and `POST /seed` system door. For real-user staging run `guide staging`;
deploy with `snapback4 deploy` + `snapback4 serve`. A deployed server (`snapback4 serve`) refuses personas and
seed, defaults to `127.0.0.1`, and requires `--i-understand-cleartext` for a non-loopback HTTP bind. Put a TLS
terminator in front, preserve `Authorization` and `Origin`, strip persona headers, and rate-limit there by the
actual client address; the binary trusts only its socket peer.

`GET /auth/config` is the small public advertisement for guest/password support. Catalog, schema, generated API,
native module and changes require a live principal. Signup requires at least 8 characters (Unicode scalar
values) and at most 1,024 UTF-8 bytes; login accepts at most 1,024 UTF-8 bytes. Password hashing runs in bounded
concurrent slots outside the engine mutex. Under `serve`, guest, login and signup share 30 attempts per socket
peer per minute, while login and signup also share 10 per normalized email per minute (E_RATE_LIMIT). Under
`dev`, loopback peers have neither minute counter; their Argon2 slots equal the host's available cores, capped
at 16 and never below 2. Startup prints the slot count and the lifted counters once. `serve` and non-loopback
peers keep two slots and the minute counters; full slots refuse with retryable E_RATE_LIMIT. Connection/worker
caps remain. Unknown emails perform the same Argon2 verification work. Retry after the minute counter or
concurrent-slot pressure clears; do not retry in a tight loop.

Use `snapback4 dev --replay` when new effects must be never delivered: writes and meters remain,
new effects are counted as suppressed, and schedule admission is held. Previously queued effects remain.
`--hold-effects` instead keeps effects claimable after restarting without the flag; `--hold-schedules`
only holds schedule admission. Both schedule holds skip missed periods; a restart without `--replay` restores normal emission for new writes and keeps the count.
The policy persists for local `run` until the next host start; suppressed effects never return.
`serve` and release commands refuse `--replay` with E_REPLAY_DEV_ONLY; production always emits normally.
See `guide jobs` for full replay semantics and the disposable-store recipe.

`snapback4 sql "SELECT * FROM messages" [--limit N] [--json]` inspects the developer's own dev store; `--release
dir` selects a deployment. It opens read-only alongside the running server and is never rule-filtered. Only
application-table views are exposed; internal tables and raw r/i are refused. Views and `data` include absent
required literal defaults; optional columns stay null. Device persistence exports retain raw storage images. The
default SELECT limit is 200, with a notice when more rows exist. `snapback4 sql '\dt'` (or `'.tables'`) lists
application tables and maintained views; `sql '\d messages'` (or `'.schema messages'`) describes Quarry
columns/nullability, indexes and unique keys. Both report the active schema generation and bounded stored row
counts: exact through 10,000, otherwise >=10,001; ephemeral counts are unavailable. `--json` returns
`generation`, `tables` and `ruleFiltered: false`. These catalog commands use the active schema, even with broken
local source. Derived sweeps use shortest text/list arguments; test.cases.jsonl supplies boundaries.

## Refusals
E_SQL_READ_ONLY means inspection received something other than one read-only `SELECT` or `WITH ... SELECT`. For
example, `snapback4 sql "DELETE FROM messages"` cannot delete rows. Use `snapback4 sql "SELECT * FROM messages"`
to inspect them, and a declared mutation for an application write. Do not combine statements or use attachment,
pragmas or side-effect functions through this inspection verb.

E_SQL_INTERNAL means inspection tried to read engine-private storage. For example, `SELECT * FROM r` or `SELECT
* FROM sqlite_master` is not an application-table view. `\dt` lists tables; `\d messages` describes one: use
`snapback4 sql '\dt'` or `snapback4 sql '\d messages'`. These views expose the developer's store, including
interpreted defaults, and are not an application authorization boundary. Use Quarry for application reads."##;

     1	//! A declared route authenticates each delivery before joining durable work.
     2	//! Unverified bytes have a bounded, ephemeral ticket, never a database seat.
     3	use super::*;
     4	use snapback4_core::{engine::{actions::ActionRequest, routes::verification_token}, schema::{RouteSpec, routes::{RouteRequest, RouteResponse, BODY_BYTES}}, value::encode_bytes_hex};
     5	
     6	#[path = "serve_route_verify.rs"]
     7	mod verification;
     8	pub(super) use verification::{claim, settle};
     9	
    10	#[derive(Default)]
    11	pub(super) struct Verification { tickets: Mutex<BTreeMap<String, Ticket>> }
    12	struct Ticket {
    13	    name: String,
    14	    contract: String,
    15	    generation: i64,
    16	    envelope: RouteRequest,
    17	    deadline: Instant,
    18	    deadline_ms: i64,
    19	    lease: Option<Lease>,
    20	    outcome: Option<Verified>,
    21	}
    22	struct Lease { worker: String, token: String, until: Instant }
    23	#[derive(Clone)]
    24	enum Verified { Allowed { id: String, principal: String }, Denied, Unavailable }
    25	struct TicketGuard<'a> { registry: &'a Verification, id: String }
    26	impl Drop for TicketGuard<'_> {
    27	    fn drop(&mut self) { lock(&self.registry.tickets).remove(&self.id); }
    28	}
    29	
    30	pub(super) fn declared(shared: &Shared, url: &str) -> Option<(String, RouteSpec)> {
    31	    lock(&shared.bound).schema.routes.iter().find(|(_, route)| route.path == url).map(|(name, route)| (name.clone(), route.clone()))
    32	}
    33	
    34	fn refuse(request: Request, status: u16, code: &'static str, message: &str) {
    35	    let mut why = Refusal::new(code, "route", message).guide("routes");
    36	    why.retryable = status >= 500;
    37	    respond_with_cors(request, status, json!({ "denied": why }), false);
    38	}
    39	fn unavailable(request: Request) {
    40	    refuse(request, 503, "E_ROUTE_PENDING", "the route has no settled response; retry the same body");
    41	}
    42	fn pause(shared: &Shared, deadline: Instant) {
    43	    let seq = lock(&shared.seq);
    44	    let _ = shared.committed.wait_timeout(seq, deadline.saturating_duration_since(Instant::now()).min(Duration::from_millis(100))).unwrap_or_else(|p| p.into_inner());
    45	}
    46	
    47	/// Bearer sessions are valid on declared public routes even in dev. Personas
    48	/// still pass through the full loopback Host/Origin/peer checks.
    49	fn principal(request: &Request, shared: &Shared) -> Result<String, Refusal> {
    50	    if !matches!(single_header(request, "x-snapback-persona"), Ok(None)) { return principal_of(request, shared).map(|c| c.0); }
    51	    let token = crate::auth::bearer_token(request).map_err(|e| e.refusal())?;
    52	    let engine = lock(&shared.engine);
    53	    snapback4_core::identity::resolve_supervised(engine.store(), &token, snapback4_core::identity::ServiceSample { now: shared.now(), supervisor_revision: shared.worker_revision.load(Ordering::Acquire) }).map_err(|e| e.refusal())?
    54	        .map(|(principal, _, _)| principal).ok_or_else(|| snapback4_core::identity::diagnostics::refused(engine.store(), &token, shared.now(), trusted_dev_request(request, shared)))
    55	}
    56	
    57	// @ref LLP 3000.009#5.1 — every provider delivery verifies before durable admission or result collection.
    58	pub(super) fn ingress(mut request: Request, shared: &Shared, name: &str, route: RouteSpec) {
    59	    let deadline = Instant::now() + Duration::from_millis(route.wait_ms);
    60	    let deadline_ms = shared.now().saturating_add(route.wait_ms as i64);
    61	    let contract = route.contract(name);
    62	    if route.service.is_none() && let Err(why) = principal(&request, shared) {
    63	        return respond_with_cors(request, 401, json!({ "denied": why }), false);
    64	    }
    65	    // A persona is never a provider credential, even on loopback.
    66	    if route.service.is_some() && !matches!(single_header(&request, "x-snapback-persona"), Ok(None)) {
    67	        return refuse(request, 401, "E_ROUTE_VERIFY", "a provider route requires its signature, not a development persona");
    68	    }
    69	    let mut envelope = RouteRequest { method: "POST".into(), path: route.path.clone(),
    70	        headers: request.headers().iter().map(|h| (h.field.as_str().to_string(), h.value.as_str().to_owned())).collect(), body_hex: String::new() };
    71	    if envelope.validate(&route).is_err() { return refuse(request, 431, "E_ROUTE_INPUT", "route headers exceed their bound or are invalid"); }
    72	    let expected = request.body_length();
    73	    if expected.is_some_and(|n| n > BODY_BYTES) { return refuse(request, 413, "E_ROUTE_INPUT", "a route body exceeds 64 KiB"); }
    74	    let mut bytes = Vec::new();
    75	    let mut buffer = [0; 8192];
    76	    loop {
    77	        let width = buffer.len().min(BODY_BYTES - bytes.len() + 1);
    78	        match request.as_reader().read(&mut buffer[..width]) {
    79	            Ok(0) => break,
    80	            Ok(n) if n > BODY_BYTES - bytes.len() => return refuse(request, 413, "E_ROUTE_INPUT", "a route body exceeds 64 KiB"),
    81	            Ok(n) => bytes.extend_from_slice(&buffer[..n]),
    82	            Err(e) if e.kind() == io::ErrorKind::Interrupted => continue,
    83	            Err(_) => return refuse(request, 400, "E_ROUTE_INPUT", "the route body could not be read"),
    84	        }
    85	    }
    86	    if expected.is_some_and(|n| n != bytes.len()) { return refuse(request, 400, "E_ROUTE_INPUT", "the route body ended before Content-Length"); }
    87	    envelope.body_hex = encode_bytes_hex(&bytes);
    88	    drop(bytes);
    89	    if Instant::now() >= deadline || shared.now() >= deadline_ms { return unavailable(request); }
    90	    let (mut run, principal) = if route.service.is_some() {
    91	        let authority = {let engine=lock(&shared.engine);crate::release_requests::stamp(engine.generation());engine.route_service_generation(name, &contract)};
    92	        let generation = match authority {
    93	            Ok((_, generation)) => generation, Err(_) => return unavailable(request),
    94	        };
    95	        let id = match verification_token() { Ok(id) => id, Err(_) => return unavailable(request) };
    96	        {
    97	            let mut tickets = lock(&shared.route_verification.tickets);
    98	            if tickets.len() >= ROUTE_WORKERS { drop(tickets); return unavailable(request); }
    99	            tickets.insert(id.clone(), Ticket { name: name.into(), contract: contract.clone(), generation, envelope,
   100	                deadline, deadline_ms, lease: None, outcome: None });
   101	        }
   102	        let guard = TicketGuard { registry: &shared.route_verification, id };
   103	        shared.durable_committed(&lock(&shared.engine));
   104	        let (id, principal) = loop {
   105	            let outcome = lock(&shared.route_verification.tickets).get(&guard.id).and_then(|ticket| ticket.outcome.clone());
   106	            match outcome {
   107	                Some(Verified::Allowed { id, principal }) => break (id, principal),
   108	                Some(Verified::Denied) => return refuse(request, 403, "E_ROUTE_VERIFY", "the provider signature was refused"),
   109	                Some(Verified::Unavailable) => return unavailable(request),
   110	                None if Instant::now() >= deadline || shared.now() >= deadline_ms || crate::lifecycle::stopping() => return unavailable(request),
   111	                None => pause(shared, deadline),
   112	            }
   113	        };
   114	        drop(guard);
   115	        let collected = {let mut engine=lock(&shared.engine);crate::release_requests::stamp(engine.generation());engine.route_request_at(name, &contract, &id, &principal, shared.now())};
   116	        match collected { Ok(run) => (run, principal), Err(_) => return unavailable(request) }
   117	    } else {
   118	        let principal = match principal(&request, shared) { Ok(p) => p, Err(why) => return respond_with_cors(request, 401, json!({ "denied": why }), false) };
   119	        let mut context = Context::new(&principal, BTreeMap::new()).at(shared.now());
   120	        if let Ok(token) = crate::auth::bearer_token(&request) { context = context.with_session(token); }
   121	        let admitted = {let mut engine=lock(&shared.engine);crate::release_requests::stamp(engine.generation());engine.request_session_route(name, &contract, &envelope, context)};
   122	        match admitted { Ok((run, _)) => (run, principal), Err(error) => return respond_with_cors(request, if error.refusal().family == "auth" { 401 } else { 503 }, json!({ "denied": error.refusal() }), false) }
   123	    };
   124	    shared.durable_committed(&lock(&shared.engine));
   125	    loop {
   126	        if route.service.is_none() && let Err(why) = self::principal(&request, shared) {
   127	            return respond_with_cors(request, 401, json!({ "denied": why }), false);
   128	        }
   129	        // Recheck even an immediately settled replay: revocation and adoption
   130	        // must not turn a retained response into a new authority grant.
   131	        let collected = {let mut engine=lock(&shared.engine);crate::release_requests::stamp(engine.generation());engine.route_request_at(name, &contract, &run.id, &principal, shared.now())};
   132	        run = match collected {
   133	            Ok(run) => run, Err(_) => return unavailable(request),
   134	        };
   135	        if run.state == "done" { return completed(request, &run); }
   136	        if matches!(run.state.as_str(), "failed" | "parked") || Instant::now() >= deadline || shared.now() >= deadline_ms || crate::lifecycle::stopping() { return unavailable(request); }
   137	        pause(shared, deadline);
   138	    }
   139	}
   140	
   141	fn completed(request: Request, run: &ActionRequest) {
   142	    let response = run.result.clone().and_then(|value| serde_json::from_value::<RouteResponse>(value).ok());
   143	    let Some(response) = response else { return unavailable(request); };
   144	    let Ok(bytes) = response.validate() else { return unavailable(request); };
   145	    if response.status >= 500 { return unavailable(request); }
   146	    let mut output = Response::from_data(bytes).with_status_code(response.status);
   147	    if let Some(header)=crate::serve::generation_header(){output.add_header(header);}
   148	    for (name, value) in response.headers {
   149	        let Ok(header) = Header::from_bytes(name, value) else { return unavailable(request); };
   150	        output.add_header(header);
   151	    }
   152	    // No JSON re-encoding, credential reflection or implicit CORS: headers
   153	    // and bytes belong to the declared handler, framing to the HTTP server.
   154	    if let Some(header) = super::latency::header() { output.add_header(header); }
   155	    let _ = request.respond_checked_with_completion(output, |result| super::latency::finished(result.is_err()));
   156	}

     1	//! Store-neutral authority decisions over typed transaction capabilities.
     2	//!
     3	//! Storage adapters expose identity and work facts. This module alone walks
     4	//! admission families and decides whether a session or claim still stands.
     5	
     6	use crate::RuntimeError;

    11	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
    12	pub struct Authority {
    13	    pub family: String,
    14	    pub generation: i64,
    15	    pub expires_at: i64,
    16	}
